# Naming pass

Tracks every name we've decided on by hand, versus the placeholder names still in the code. The
placeholders are address names like `func_ov009_02188e70`, or guessed names like `SyncEntryPairAndRefreshFlags_02188454`.

- **Decided** means it's applied in the code and renamed in `symbols.txt` for usa, eur and jpn.
- **Placeholder** means it still has its old name. The suggested names below aren't decided yet.

**Every time something is named, update this file in the same commit**: move its row from Placeholders to
Decided, or add a row. `python tools/naming_check.py` fails when a renamed function is missing from the
Decided table, and the local pre-commit hook runs it.

## Conventions

- **Functions:** `Owner_MethodName`. The owner is the class the function will belong to, followed by the
  method name in PascalCase, for example `CharacterCreationUI_LoadSlotLayout`. Once we move to real classes
  the `_` becomes `::`, giving `CharacterCreationUI::LoadSlotLayout()`.
- **Types:** use the prefix of the header they live in. Character creation types are `Creation...`, like
  `CreationNavigation` and `CreationSlotLayout`.
- **Classes that already exist** (like `GameState` in `include/GameState/GameState.h`): make the function a real
  member now, for example `GameState::GetResources()`, instead of `GameState_GetResources`.
- **Data:** `Owner_camelCaseName`, for example `CharacterCreationUI_slotLayouts`.
- **Origin comment:** every renamed function gets this directly above it:
  `// Overlay N, originally func_... (USA/EUR addr, JPN addr)`. Use `Main` for arm9 main functions.
- **Vocabulary for character creation:** a *tab* is one page (gender, body type, ...). A *slot* is a
  position on a tab you can pick: a button, swatch or key. A *choice* is the value picked (`bodyType[sex]`).

It does the same for the six style scores (from Charm plus each item's style points; the best is Style: LIVE 520 = 500 + 20, not element resistances) and adds the attack/defence growth and weapon bonuses. With out == NULL it writes the results (stats, max HP/MP, attack, defence, resistances). With `out` it substitutes out->record into its equipment slot and only reports the would-be values (an equip preview). Read from code: CharacterCreationUI_HandleConfirm calls it with out = 0 right after copying the appearance items into member->equipment, then reads member->stats into the record. The 11 records at +0x194 are PartyMemberData::equipment (NAMING row 735, which already notes this function walks records[11]). (LIVE: In a shop's equip preview, log func_02083e28 with a non-NULL second argument.) LIVE (base stats 100..900): the nine stats are, in base/stats order, Strength, Agility, Resilience, Deftness, Charm, Magical Mending, Magical Might, Max HP, Max MP; Attack = Strength + weapon, Defence = Resilience + armour; it does not run during an equip, only when a screen or battle needs the stats. |

| `ItemTable_CopyIds` | `func_020def98` | Main | `0x020def98` (EUR `0x020defa8`) | `0x020e0b10` | Fills an item table (dest) with the records of the given ids found in a source item table. Each id is found by BinarySearchByComparator020de650 on the record id (+0x18). With an allocator, it skips negative, missing and repeated ids, allocates (count + n) 0x20-byte records plus room for the name strings, copies with the strings (CopyEntryAndAllocateStrings020df618), then sorts the result by id with a cocktail sort. Without an allocator, it copies into dest's existing entries position by position (CopyEntryAndIncrementCounter020df6cc), so a missing id leaves a gap. It sets count, capacity and name counts, clears the owned bit and resets the unused tail entries. It returns 1 on success, or 0 for bad arguments. Read from code: ItemTable_LoadIds (decided) calls it as (allocator, table, fileTable, ids, count), where fileTable comes from ItemTable_LoadInPlace on the item data. It then sets the binary-search flag when count > 1, which works because the result is sorted by id. |

| `Vector3fix_ClosestPointOnTriangle` | `func_02031d20` | Main | `0x02031d20` (EUR `0x02031d30`) | `0x02031858` | Returns the point of triangle (a, b, c) closest to `point`, via the Voronoi regions: a vertex, or a point on edge ab, ac or bc. If none of those regions apply it returns `point` unchanged (no plane projection). Read from code: The body is the standard closest-point-on-triangle region test with the dot products d1–d6 and the barycentric checks vc, vb, va, using Vector3fix_Subtract, Vector3fix_InnerProduct and fix32_Divide. Its only caller is ZoneTerrain_PushOutOfWalls' second pass, when the segment test returned -1 (the point's projection is outside the triangle). |
| `Vector3fix_IntersectSegmentTriangle` | `func_02031b84` | Main | `0x02031b84` (EUR `0x02031b94`) | `0x020316bc` | Segment start→end against triangle (a, b, c). It returns 0 if the segment doesn't cross the triangle's plane front-to-back, -1 if it crosses the plane outside the triangle, and 1 on a hit. On a hit it writes the barycentric weights (A, B, C) and the parameter t along the segment, all fx32. The normal argument is unused. Read from code: It computes the normal = ab × ac and the determinant from (start − end)·normal, rejects ≤ 0 and t outside [0, det], and computes the weights from (dir × rel)·ac and ·ab, scaled by 1/det. ZoneTerrain_FindFloorHit and ZoneTerrain_FindWallHit use result 1 and the weights for the hit height. |

| `fix32_SinFx64c` | `func_020c3260` | Main | `0x020c3260` (EUR `0x020c3270`) | `0x020c4d2c` | sin of an fx32 angle in radians, returned with 32 fractional bits (fx64c). The function is odd: negative angles return the negated result. It scales the angle by 4/π (0x145F306DD), works out the octant, mirrors odd octants and evaluates the sine series (func_020c30ac) or the cosine series (func_020c3194) on [0, π/4]. Octants 4–7 return a negative value. Read from code: func_020c30ac is a sine series ending in 0xC90FDAA2 (π/4 in 0.32) and func_020c3194 is the cosine series. Zone3D_CollectTerrainPolygons computes `int sine = 4096 * (float)(func_020c3260(rotationAngle)) / 2^32`, and func_ov017_02193dc4 does the same. |

The function is even: a negative angle gives the same result as its absolute value. It uses the same octant scheme as fix32_SinFx64c with the series swapped, and is negative in octants 2–5. Read from code: Zone3D_CollectTerrainPolygons and func_ov017_02193dc4 compute `int cosine = 4096 * (float)(func_020c32f8(rotationAngle)) / 2^32` next to the sine. The SDK name is FX_CosFx64c. |

| `Scenario_GetInstance` | `func_0205ec34` | Main | `0x0205ec34` (EUR `0x0205ec44`) | `0x0205ff20` | Returns &g_scenario, the scenario state: the story step (+0..+2), the flag bits (+0x8c: containers' opened flags, red chests ID + 0x212, the rest ID + 0x79e, and story flags), per-step local flags (slot +0x332: A at +3, B at +0x10), the queued commands (+0x30), the zone's triggers (+0x47c) and areas (+0x494). Round 1's verifier rejected Scenario_GetInstance because the object is the event/script system; the game's own name for this data is its folder data/scenario/ (trigger%s.bin, the area .npc files). LIVE (2026-10-26, Angel Falls, story 2.1.1): every zone entry runs Scenario_LoadZoneTriggers(owner = g_scenario) from FieldTask_EnterZone+0xce0; its first bytes are the story step (02 01 01); talking to an NPC runs Scenario_RunCommand on it. |

LIVE: zone 1105's load ran 0x8f with id 15 and corners (2.65, 2.04, 5.26) / (1.35, -1.65, -5.93) = triggerM01's record; talking to an NPC ran 0x76. |

Read from code; matches triggerM01.bin's records (romdata.py triggers). |

The `CharacterModel_` functions belong to the character model in overlay 23 (10 `Object3D` parts,
`0xc20` bytes), which has no shared type yet; its per-file copies are named like `CharacterModel_021e5974`.

`GridCursor` is the generic menu grid cursor in main, used by menus across the game (character creation's copy is
`navigation`). Its other functions are still placeholders: `InitStruct_0205ba2c`, `GetField0_0205bafc`, `func_0205bf58`.

The `Keyboard_` functions belong to the software keyboard in overlay 3 that names are typed on. Each file still has
its own copy of the `Keyboard` struct.

### Types

| Name | Original | Where | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| `CreationSlotLayout` | `CreationChoiceGrid`, `Struct021847c4` | `include/Resource/CreationSlotLayout.h` | Where a tab's slots sit on the touchscreen: `x, y, width, height, stepX, stepY, columns, rows` |
| `Keyboard` | `Self0215e8f4` | `Keyboard_GetPageGrid`, `Keyboard_MoveCursor`, `Keyboard_Update` (file-local) | The keyboard: key at +0, layout at +4, page at +0x1e, wrap at +0x21. Keyboard_GetPageGrid's symbol was retyped (usa/eur) |
| `KeyboardKey` | `Key0215ec90` | same files | One key: its character `code`, its `shiftedCode`, the action byte at `+0xd` (its slot in the key action table) and `isCommand` |

### Macros

| Name | Original | In | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `MOVE_CURSOR_TO_TAB_ICON` | `TAB_CURSOR` | `CharacterCreationUI_HandleInput` (both halves) | Puts the 16×16 highlight on tab icon N in the top row (`x = 0x78 + N × 16`, `y = 8`) |
| `RESET_KEYBOARD_NAVIGATION` | (none; the block was written out twice) | `CharacterCreationUI_HandleNameEntry` | Puts `self`'s `GridCursor` back on the first cell of the 11 × 6 keyboard grid. It keeps its own `navigation` local: passing `&self->navigation_` straight to the calls compiles differently |
| `DISPCNT_MASK_DISPLAY`, `DISPCNT_DISPLAY_BG0`, `_BG1`, `_BG2`, `_OBJ` | `0x1f00`, `0x100`, `0x200`, `0x400`, `0x1000` | `CharacterCreationUI_ApplyTabGraphics` (file-local; belongs in `NitroSDK/Graphics.h` beside `DISPCNT_MASK_*`) | DISPCNT bits 8–12 select which of BG0–BG3 and the sprites are displayed (GBATEK; NitroSDK's `GX_PLANEMASK_*` << 8). This function sets the sub engine to `0x1500` = BG0 + BG2 + OBJ while a tab loads, so sub BG1 (the selection layer, `tabBgNumbers[1]` = 1) is hidden. It sets `0x1700` = BG0 + BG1 + BG2 + OBJ once the tab is built |
| `RESET_NAVIGATION(columns, rows, count, index)` | (same name; parameters `total`, `selected`) | `CharacterCreationUI_HandleInput` (both halves) | Rebuilds `self->navigation` as a one-page, row-major `columns × rows` grid of `count` cells on cell `index`, with no row wrap. Used for the tab icon row (Up from row 0) and, on name entry, for the keyboard (Down from the icons) |
| `MOVE_CURSOR_TO_TAB_ICON(icon)` | parameter `selected` | same | Now written with `CreationTabIcon_*` |
| `KEY_DPAD` | `0xf0`, `KEY_RIGHT \| KEY_LEFT \| KEY_UP \| KEY_DOWN` | `include/Input/Input.h` | The four D-pad bits. Used in GridCursor_Update and the TestKeys*(0xf0) calls of func_ov017_02197a64, func_020440cc, func_ov002_0216acf0, func_ov002_021665b0 |
| `REG_BLDCNT`, `REG_BLDCNT_SUB` | `0x4000050`; local defines in HandleConfirm, HandleNewGameFinish, HandleClose, func_ov023_021ec480 | `include/NitroSDK/HWRegisters.h` | The main/sub engine blend control addresses the ColorEffect_* functions take (ColorEffects.h), which also set BLDALPHA after them |
